lies dense in
\mathcal{H}
.

Proof
.

Ad

(a)
. Let
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
be arbitrary. For all
A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
, there holds

\|A(A^{\prime}\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}}=\|A^{\prime}(A\xi)\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\|A^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|A\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\|A^{\prime}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,C_{\xi}\,\|\eta_{\psi}(A)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}\ .

Ad

(b)
. Let
P\in\mathscr{P}(\mathcal{H})
denote the projection onto
\clos_{\|\cdot\|}\bigl(D(\mathcal{H},\psi)\bigr)
. It will be shown that
P=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
; the assertion then follows from
Corollary

B.1.10

(b)
. First, observe that the invariance of the lineal under the commutant implies that
PA^{\prime}P=A^{\prime}P=A^{\prime}PP
for all
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. This shows that actually
P\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime\prime}=\mathfrak{M}
. Next, because
\psi
is a normal weight,
Theorem

III.4.4
implies that
\psi=\sum_{i\in I}\omega_{\xi_{i}}
for some family
(\xi_{i})_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
. For every
i\in I
, there holds
\xi_{i}\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
; this follows from the representation of
\psi
and noting that for all
A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
,

\|A\xi_{i}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\langle{\xi_{i},A^{\ast}A\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\psi(A^{\ast}A)=\|\eta_{\psi}(A)\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}^{2}\ .

(III.14)

Therefore,
P\xi_{i}=\xi_{i}
for all
i\in I
by definition of
P
, and so one obtains that
\psi(\mathord{\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}}-P)=0
. As
\psi
was assumed to be faithful, it follows that
P=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
. (Note that the previous observation
P\in\mathfrak{M}
was used in order to apply the weight
\psi
to the element
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-P\in\mathfrak{M}^{+}
.)

The next result, mentioned but not proved in
[
118
, p. 69]
, will be required down the line to establish another result which will find crucial application in
Chapter

IV
.

Lemma III.4.7
.

If
\psi=\omega_{\varPsi}
is a vector functional on
\mathfrak{M}
induced by
\varPsi\in\mathcal{H}
, then
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\varPsi
.

Proof
.

1.
Note that
Eq.

III.14
in the previous proof shows that the vector
\varPsi
is contained in the lineal of the state
\psi
it induces, and since
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
is invariant under the commutant of
\mathfrak{M}
by
Proposition

III.4.6

(a)
, it follows that
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\varPsi\subseteq D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
.

2.
Let
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
be arbitrary, that is,
\|A\xi\|^{2}\leq C_{\xi}\,\|A\varPsi\|^{2}
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}
by (
III.13
). Consider the subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}\coloneqq[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]
and
\mathcal{H}_{\xi}\coloneqq[\mathfrak{M}\xi]
of
\mathcal{H}
; they, as well as their orthogonal complements, are invariant under
\mathfrak{M}
. Define a linear operator
T_{\xi}^{\prime}:\mathfrak{M}\varPsi\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathfrak{M}\xi
in
\mathcal{H}
by setting
T_{\xi}^{\prime}(A\varPsi)\coloneqq A\xi
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}
. The previous inequality shows that
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
is bounded, and that
A\varPsi=0
implies
T_{\xi}^{\prime}(A\varPsi)=A\xi=0
. Therefore,
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
is well-defined and extends uniquely to a linear operator
T_{\xi}^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
(
Theorem

B.1.1
). Note that if
(A_{n}\varPsi)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(B_{n}\varPsi)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
are two different sequences approximating the element
\eta\in\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}
,
i.e.
,
\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,A_{n}\varPsi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,B_{n}\varPsi=\eta
, then

\|A_{n}\xi-B_{n}\xi\|\leq\sqrt{C_{\xi}}\,\|(A_{n}-B_{n})\varPsi\|\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow 0\quad\text{as}\quad n\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow+\infty\ ,

so the definition of
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
is indeed independent of the approximating sequence. This establishes that
T_{\xi}^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{\xi}
is a well-defined bounded linear operator. Finally, since
\mathcal{H}=\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}\oplus(\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi})^{\perp}
(
Theorem

B.1.8
), one can extend the operator
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
to the whole space
\mathcal{H}
by setting
T_{\xi}^{\prime}\zeta\coloneqq 0
for all
\zeta\in(\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi})^{\perp}
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\zeta\in(\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi})^{\perp}
be arbitrary. As mentioned above, it holds that
A\zeta\in(\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi})^{\perp}
and, moreover,
AT_{\xi}^{\prime}\zeta=0=T_{\xi}^{\prime}(A\zeta)
by definition of
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
; this shows that
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
commutes with
A
on the subspace
(\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi})^{\perp}
. Let now
\eta=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,B_{n}\varPsi\in\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}
be arbitrary. Then

\displaystyle AT_{\xi}^{\prime}\eta=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,AB_{n}\xi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,T_{\xi}^{\prime}(AB_{n}\varPsi)=T_{\xi}^{\prime}(A\eta)\ ,

that is,
T_{\xi}^{\prime}
commutes with
A
on
\mathcal{H}_{\varPsi}
as well,
i.e.
,
T_{\xi}^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. By construction, there holds
\xi=T_{\xi}^{\prime}\varPsi
, whence one obtains that
\xi\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\varPsi
which concludes the proof.

III.4.b  Intertwining Representations

As mentioned in the introduction of this section, the goal is to construct an analogue of the relative modular operator which can be employed in any representation of the von Neumann algebra. Therefore, it is fruitful to study how the GNS-representation intertwines with other representations.

III.4.8
Intertwining operators.

Let
\psi
be a faithful semi-finite normal weight on the von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
, and let
(\mathcal{H}_{\psi},\pi_{\psi},\eta_{\psi})
be the GNS-representation with respect to
\psi
. For arbitrary
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
, define a linear operator
R_{\xi}^{\psi}:\mathcal{H}_{\psi}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}
by setting

R_{\xi}^{\psi}\,\eta_{\psi}(A)\coloneqq A\xi\ \quad(A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi})\ .

Since this defines a bounded operator by the definition (
III.12
) of the lineal of
\psi
, and since
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
is dense in
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
by construction (
III.4.3
), it follows that
R_{\xi}^{\psi}
extends uniquely to the whole space
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
(
Theorem

B.1.1
). The adjoint of this operator is defined by the relation
[
155
, p. 77]

\big\langle{(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\zeta,\eta_{\psi}(A)}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}=\langle{\zeta,A\xi}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\quad(\zeta\in\mathcal{H},\,A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi})\ .

For
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
, one furthermore defines a bounded self-adjoint operator
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi):\mathcal{H}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}
by

\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)\coloneqq R_{\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\ .

Observe that if
\psi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
, then
R_{\xi}^{\psi}
is again defined using the GNS-representation
(\mathcal{H}_{\psi},\pi_{\psi},\varPsi_{\psi})
associated with
\psi
: for all
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
and
A\in\mathfrak{M}
, one sets
[
118
, Eq. (4.3)]

R_{\xi}^{\psi}\bigl(\pi_{\psi}(A)\varPsi_{\psi}\bigr)\coloneqq A\xi\ .

The following proposition collects basic properties of
R_{\xi}^{\psi}
and
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)

[
83
, Lem. 11.2]
.

Proposition III.4.9
.

(a)

For every
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, it holds that
R_{A^{\prime}\xi}^{\psi}=A^{\prime}R_{\xi}^{\psi}
.

(b)

The operator
R_{\xi}^{\psi}
intertwines the GNS-representation of
\mathfrak{M}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
with the identity representation
\mathfrak{M}\owns A\varmapstochar\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
of
\mathfrak{M}
on
\mathcal{H}
:
AR_{\xi}^{\psi}=R_{\xi}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A)
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}
.

(c)

If
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
, then
R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi})^{\ast}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. In particular,
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
for all
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
.

Proof
.

Ad

(a)
. It was observed before in
Proposition

III.4.6

(a)
that for
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
and
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, also
A^{\prime}\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
. For arbitrary
X\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
, one obtains
R_{A^{\prime}\xi}^{\psi}\,\eta_{\psi}(X)=XA^{\prime}\xi=A^{\prime}X\xi=A^{\prime}R_{\xi}^{\psi}\,\eta_{\psi}(X)
, and this identity extends, by continuity, to the whole Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
.

Ad

(b)
. For every
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
,
A\in\mathfrak{M}
, and
X\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
, one finds using the definition of the representation
\pi_{\psi}
that
R_{\xi}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A)\eta_{\psi}(X)=R_{\xi}^{\psi}\eta_{\psi}(AX)=AX\xi=AR_{\xi}^{\psi}\eta_{\psi}(X)
, hence
R_{\xi}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A)=AR_{\xi}^{\psi}
on
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
which, again, extends by continuity to
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
.

Ad

(c)
. Applying the previous observation multiple times, one computes for arbitrary
A\in\mathfrak{M}
:

\displaystyle AR_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi})^{\ast}

\displaystyle=R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A)(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi})^{\ast}=R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}\Bigl(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A)^{\ast}\Bigr)^{\ast}

\displaystyle=R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}\Bigl(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi}\pi_{\psi}(A^{\ast})\Bigr)^{\ast}=R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}\Bigl(A^{\ast}R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi}\Bigr)^{\ast}=R_{\xi_{1}}^{\psi}(R_{\xi_{2}}^{\psi})^{\ast}A\ .

The next result will be a key ingredient in the proof of Uhlmann’s monotonicity theorem for the relative entropy in
Section

IV.3
. The assertion is mentioned but not proved in
[
118
, p. 70]
.

Proposition III.4.10
.

Let
\psi=\omega_{\varPsi}
be a vector functional on
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
induced by a vector
\varPsi\in\mathcal{H}
. Then it follows that for every
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, the operator
\varTheta^{\psi}(A^{\prime}\varPsi)
is given by

\varTheta^{\psi}(A^{\prime}\varPsi)=A^{\prime}\,[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi](A^{\prime})^{\ast}\ .

Proof
.

According to
Lemma

III.4.7
, it holds that
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\varPsi
, so one can indeed consider the operator
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)
for vectors
\xi=A^{\prime}\varPsi
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. (In particular,
\varPsi
is itself an element of
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
.) Using
Proposition

III.4.9

(a)
and the definition of
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)
, one finds

\displaystyle\varTheta^{\psi}(A^{\prime}\varPsi)

\displaystyle=R_{A^{\prime}\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{A^{\prime}\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}=A^{\prime}R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}\bigl(A^{\prime}R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}\bigr)^{\ast}=A^{\prime}R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}(A^{\prime})^{\ast}\ .

It remains to show that
P\coloneqq R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}
is the orthogonal projection
[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]
onto
U\coloneqq\clos_{\|\cdot\|}(\mathfrak{M}\varPsi)
. To this end, let
(\mathcal{H}_{\psi},\pi_{\psi},\varPsi_{\psi})
be the GNS-representation with respect to the functional
\psi
, and observe first that for all
A,B\in\mathfrak{M}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\big\langle{(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}A\varPsi,\pi_{\psi}(B)\varPsi_{\psi}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\big\langle{A\varPsi,R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}\bigl(\pi_{\psi}(B)\varPsi_{\psi}\bigr)}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle{A\varPsi,B\varPsi}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\psi(A^{\ast}B)=\big\langle{\varPsi_{\psi},\pi_{\psi}(A^{\ast}B)\varPsi_{\psi}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}=\langle{\pi_{\psi}(A)\varPsi_{\psi},\pi_{\psi}(B)\varPsi_{\psi}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}\ .

Since
\pi_{\psi}(\mathfrak{M})\varPsi_{\psi}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
is dense, this shows that
(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}A\varPsi=\pi_{\psi}(A)\varPsi_{\psi}
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}
, and hence it follows that
(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
. Therefore,

P^{2}=\bigl(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)^{2}=R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}=R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}=P

which shows that
P=R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}
is a projection. Since this operator is self-adjoint, it is automatically an orthogonal projection (
Definition

B.1.6
). For every
\xi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,A_{n}\varPsi\in[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]
, it holds that

R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}A_{n}\varPsi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,A_{n}\varPsi=\xi\ ,

so
P|_{U}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
which implies
U=[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]\subseteq\ran(P)
by
Lemma

B.1.7
. Moreover, for all
u^{\perp}\in U^{\perp}=[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]^{\perp}
and
B\in\mathfrak{M}
, it follows that

\big\langle{(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\,u^{\perp},\pi_{\psi}(B)\varPsi_{\psi}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}=\big\langle{u^{\perp},R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}\bigl(\pi_{\psi}(B)\varPsi_{\psi}\bigr)}\big\rangle=\langle{u^{\perp},B\varPsi}\rangle=0\ ,

thus
(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\,u^{\perp}=0
, and this implies
[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]^{\perp}\subseteq\ker(P)
. From this observation and
Lemma

B.1.11
, it follows that
\ran(P)=[\mathfrak{M}\varPsi]
, hence
P=R_{\varPsi}^{\psi}(R_{\varPsi}^{\psi})^{\ast}
is the orthogonal projection onto
U
.

III.4.c  Construction of the Spatial Derivative

The following lemma is the essential result needed for the definition of the spatial derivative as the unique self-adjoint operator associated with a suitable quadratic form
[
83
, Lem. 11.3]
,
[
155
, Sect. 7.3]
.

Lemma III.4.11
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a von Neumann algebra, let
\mathfrak{N}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be its commutant, let
\varphi
be a semi-finite normal weight on
\mathfrak{M}
, and let
\psi
be a faithful semi-finite normal weight on
\mathfrak{N}
. Define a function
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}:\mathcal{H}\supseteq\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi)\coloneqq\varphi\bigl(\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)\bigr){\ ,\quad}\xi\in\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})\coloneqq\big\{{\eta\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)\ :\ \mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\eta)<+\infty}\big\}\ .

Then
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is a densely defined, lower semi-continuous, positive quadratic form on
\mathcal{H}
, cf.
Definition

C.1.1
.

Proof
.

1.
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is a positive quadratic form
. Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}
and
\xi,\eta\in\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})=D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
be arbitrary. Using
Proposition

III.4.9

(a)
, one finds that

\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\lambda\xi)=\varphi\bigl(\varTheta^{\psi}(\lambda\xi)\bigr)=\varphi\bigl(R_{\lambda\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\lambda\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)=\varphi\bigl(|\lambda|^{2}R_{\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)=|\lambda|^{2}\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi)\ .

Similarly, one computes

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi+\eta)+\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi-\eta)

\displaystyle=\varphi\bigl(\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi+\eta)\bigr)+\varphi\bigl(\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi-\eta)\bigr)=\varphi\bigl(R_{\xi+\eta}^{\psi}(R_{\xi+\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)+\varphi\bigl(R_{\xi-\eta}^{\psi}(R_{\xi-\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\varphi\bigl((R_{\xi}^{\psi}+R_{\eta}^{\psi})(R_{\xi}^{\psi}+R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)+\varphi\bigl((R_{\xi}^{\psi}-R_{\eta}^{\psi})(R_{\xi}^{\psi}-R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\varphi\bigl(R_{\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)+2\varphi\bigl(R_{\eta}^{\psi}(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)

\displaystyle=2\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi)+2\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\eta)\ .

Furthermore,
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is positive because
\varTheta^{\psi}(\xi)
is a positive operator by construction, and
\varphi
is, as a weight, positive as well. This shows that
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is a quadratic form in the sense of
Definition

C.1.1
.

2.
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is densely defined
. Since
\psi
is faithful, it follows from
Proposition

III.4.6

(b)
that
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)\subseteq\mathcal{H}
is dense. Moreover, because
\varphi
is semi-finite, the left ideal
\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi}
is dense in
\mathfrak{M}
with respect to the
\sigma
-weak operator topology by
Definition

III.4.1
. As the algebra involution is
\sigma
-weakly continuous (
II.3.3
), it follows that the set
\big\{{A\eta\,:\,A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi}^{\ast},\,\eta\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)}\big\}
is dense in
\mathcal{H}
with respect to the norm topology
[
155
, p. 79]
and contained in
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
because the lineal of
\psi
is invariant under
\mathfrak{N}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime\prime}=\mathfrak{M}
by
Proposition

III.4.6

(a)
. Furthermore, for any
A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\varphi}^{\ast}
and
\xi\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
, one finds that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(A\xi)=\varphi\bigl(R_{A\xi}^{\psi}(R_{A\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\bigr)=\varphi\bigl(AR_{\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}A^{\ast}\bigr)\leq\|R_{\xi}^{\psi}(R_{\xi}^{\psi})^{\ast}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\varphi(AA^{\ast})<+\infty

by using
Proposition

III.4.9

(a)
and
Eq.

III.11
, hence
A\xi\in\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})
. This shows that
\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})
contains a norm-dense subset, and so it is itself dense in
\mathcal{H}
.

3.
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is lower semi-continuous on
D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
. By
Theorem

III.4.4
, there exists a family of vectors
(\xi_{i})_{i\in I}\subseteq\mathcal{H}
such that
\varphi=\sum_{i\in I}\omega_{\xi_{i}}
. Furthermore, note that by the Hahn-Banach theorem
[
171
, Cor. III.1.7]
and the representation theorem of Fréchet-Riesz
[
171
, Thm. V.3.6]
,

\|\zeta\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}=\sup_{f\in(\mathcal{H}_{\psi})^{\ast},\,\|f\|\leq 1}|f(\zeta)|=\sup_{\xi\in\mathcal{H}_{\psi},\,\|\xi\|\leq 1}|\langle{\xi,\zeta}\rangle|=\sup_{A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi},\,\|A\|\leq 1}|\langle{\eta_{\psi}(A),\zeta}\rangle|

for all
\zeta\in\mathcal{H}_{\psi}
, where in the last step, it was used that
\mathcal{H}_{\psi}=\clos_{\|\cdot\|}(\mathfrak{N}_{\psi})
by the GNS-construction, hence it suffices to take the supremum over elements from
\mathfrak{N}_{\psi}
. With this, one obtains for all
\eta\in D(\mathcal{H},\psi)
and fixed
i\in I
that

\omega_{\xi_{i}}\bigl(\varTheta^{\psi}(\eta)\bigr)=\big\langle{\xi_{i},R_{\eta}^{\psi}(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\big\|{(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}^{2}=\sup_{A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi},\,\|A\|\leq 1}\big|{\big\langle{\eta_{\psi}(A),(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\big\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}}\big|^{2}\ .

Since
\langle{\eta_{\psi}(A),(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\psi}}=\langle{A\eta,\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\langle{\eta,A^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
, it follows that
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\eta)
takes the form

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\eta)=\sum_{i\in I}\langle{\xi_{i},R_{\eta}^{\psi}(R_{\eta}^{\psi})^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=\sum_{i\in I}\sup_{A\in\mathfrak{N}_{\psi},\,\|A\|\leq 1}\big|{\langle{\eta,A^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}}\big|^{2}\ .

The function
\mathcal{H}\supseteq D(\mathcal{H},\psi)\owns\eta\varmapstochar\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow|\langle{\eta,A^{\ast}\xi_{i}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|^{2}\in\mathbb{R}
is continuous, so in particular lower semi-continuous. As the supremum and the sum of lower semi-continuous functions are again lower semi-continuous
[
117
, Cor. 3.2.8]
, it follows that
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is lower semi-continuous.

III.4.12
The spatial derivative operator.

(
[
83
, Def. 11.4]
)
Let
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a von Neumann algebra,
\mathfrak{N}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be its commutant, and
\psi
be a faithful semi-finite normal weight on
\mathfrak{N}
. For any semi-finite normal weight
\varphi
on
\mathfrak{M}
, let
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
be the densely defined positive quadratic form from above.

By
Lemma

III.4.11
and
Proposition

C.1.4
, it follows that
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
is a closable form, hence the closure
\overline{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}
of
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
exists which is a closed positive quadratic form and the smallest closed extension of
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
(
cf.

C.1.5
). Applying the form representation theorem,
Theorem

C.2.2
(see also
C.2.3
), one obtains a uniquely defined positive self-adjoint operator
Q_{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}
associated with
\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}
which satisfies

\dom\bigl(Q_{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}^{1/2}\bigr)=\dom(\overline{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}})\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}(\xi)=\big\|{Q_{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}^{1/2}\xi}\big\|^{2}{\ ,\quad}\xi\in\dom(\overline{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}})\ .

This
Q_{\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}}
is called the
spatial derivative operator
of the semi-finite normal weight
\varphi
on
\mathfrak{M}
with respect to the faithful semi-finite normal weight
\psi
on
\mathfrak{N}=\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, and it will be denoted by

\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)\quad\text{or}\quad\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\psi}}\ .

According to the discussion in
C.2.3
, it holds that
\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)
is the largest positive self-adjoint operator, with respect to the ordering defined in
C.2.1
, such that

\dom\bigl(\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)^{1/2}\bigr)\supseteq\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi}(\xi)=\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)^{1/2}\xi}\big\|^{2}{\ ,\quad}\xi\in\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})\ .

(III.15)

Moreover, the form domain
\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})
is a core for
\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)^{1/2}
, and if
\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
is a normal functional, then

D(\mathcal{H},\psi)=\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi})\subseteq\dom\bigl(\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)^{1/2}\bigr)\ .

Finally, one can show that the support of
\varDelta(\varphi/\psi)
is given by
\mathbf{s}(\varphi)

[
83
, Thm. 11.7 (3)]
.

The following proposition shows that the spatial derivative is a generalization of the relative modular operator to the setting in which the von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
is represented on an arbitrary Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, not necessarily the standard representation
[
83
, p. 183]
. In particular, it follows that the examples from
Examples

III.3.4
also apply to the spatial derivative. The proof of the assertion may be found in
[
83
, Prop. 11.6]
.

Proposition III.4.13
.

Let
(\mathfrak{M},\mathcal{H},J,\mathcal{P})
be a von Neumann algebra in standard form, and let
\varphi,\psi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
with
\psi
faithful. Define a faithful
\psi^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime})_{\ast}^{+}
by
\psi^{\prime}(A^{\prime})\coloneqq\psi(J(A^{\prime})^{\ast}J)
for
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, that is,
\psi^{\prime}(A^{\prime})=\langle{\varPsi,A^{\prime}\varPsi}\rangle
, where
\varPsi\in\mathcal{P}
is the vector representative of
\psi
. Then

\varDelta(\varphi/\psi^{\prime})=\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}\ .

Chapter IV
The Araki-Uhlmann Relative Entropy

In the previous two chapters, the theory of operator algebras and modular theory in von Neumann algebras were outlined very roughly. Now, the focus of the presentation shifts towards applications of this theory to the study of the relative entropy functional. In
Section

IV.1
, the
Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy
is defined for von Neumann algebras in standard form, and its most important properties are proved.
Section

IV.2
discusses the same functional for arbitrary representations of the von Neumann algebra; both definitions are useful in different situations, and both will be employed in later investigations in this text. The main part of this chapter is
Section

IV.3
, where
Uhlmann’s monotonicity theorem
for the relative entropy is proved in great detail, and some of its corollaries are discussed. Finally,
Section

IV.4
contains some original results: Uhlmann’s theorem is applied to the specific situation of two vector functionals in order to find monotonicity inequalities with respect to certain Hilbert-space transformations.

References
.

The main sources are
[
118
, Ch. 5]
and
[
10
,
11
,
122
,
123
]
.

IV.1
von Neumann Algebras in Standard Form

In the following, a generalization of the Umegaki relative entropy,
cf.

Eq.

I.2
, to normal functionals on general von Neumann algebras will be given. It will be assumed that the von Neumann algebra is represented in standard form (
cf.

Section

III.2
). In this case, the relative entropy was defined for faithful normal states by
H. Araki
in 1976
[
10
]
using the relative modular operator (
cf.

Section

III.3
). Subsequently, in 1977 he generalized his formula to non-faithful states in the seminal work
[
11
]
.

IV.1.a  Definition and Examples

Definition IV.1.1
(Relative entropy – standard form).

Let
(\mathfrak{M},\mathcal{H},J,\mathcal{P})
be a von Neumann algebra in standard form, let
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
be two positive normal functionals, and let
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
be the corresponding vector representatives of these functionals in the natural positive cone (
Theorem

III.2.8
). The
Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy
of
\psi
and
\varphi
is defined to be

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\big\rangle&\text{if $\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)$}\ ,\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise}\ .\end{cases}

(IV.1)

Remark IV.1.2
.

The relation
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
is equivalent to the condition
\varPsi\in\supp(\varphi)
. Indeed, since
\supp(\psi)=\ran\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)=[\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}\varPsi]
according to
Proposition

II.4.13
, it is clear that the former implies the latter. For the converse implication, let
\xi\in\supp(\psi)
be arbitrary,
i.e.
,
\xi=\operatorname*{\text{$\mathcal{H}$}-lim}_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,A_{n}^{\prime}\varPsi
for some
(A_{n}^{\prime})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. As the assumption
\varPsi\in\supp(\varphi)
implies
\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\varPsi=\varPsi
(
Lemma

B.1.7
), one also obtains

\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\xi=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbf{s}(\varphi)A_{n}^{\prime}\varPsi=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}A_{n}^{\prime}\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\varPsi=\xi\ ,

where in the second step
\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\in\mathfrak{M}
was used,
cf.

II.4.9
. This shows that
\xi\in\ran\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\bigr)=\supp(\varphi)
by the aforementioned lemma, hence
\supp\psi\subseteq\supp\varphi
.

The following representation of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
is given in
[
11
, Def. 3.1]
as a definition.

Lemma IV.1.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
,
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
, and
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
be as above. Denote by
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}
the unique spectral measure associated with the positive self-adjoint relative modular operator
\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}
. In case that
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
, the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
can be written equivalently as

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\log(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle\ .

(IV.2)

Proof
.

Recall from
Proposition

III.3.5

(c)
the relation
\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1}=J\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}J
which holds with restriction to
\supp(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
, that is,
\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})=J\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}J\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
, where
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})=\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{\varphi}
is the support projection of the relative modular operator according to
Proposition

III.3.5

(a)
. Taking the logarithm of
\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1}
and using properties of the functional calculus, one obtains (see
Lemma

B.3.5
and
[
11
, Rem. 3.4]
)

\log(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})=-J\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})J\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\ .

By the assumption
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
, it holds that
\varPsi\in\supp(\varphi)
according to
Remark

IV.1.2
. Therefore,
\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\varPsi=\varPsi
by
Lemma

B.1.7
. Using also
\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{\varphi}=\mathbf{s}(\psi)J\mathbf{s}(\varphi)J
(
Lemma

III.3.1
), it follows that
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi=\varPsi
because
J\varPsi=\varPsi
since
\varPsi\in\mathcal{P}
, see
Proposition

III.2.2

(b)
. Therefore, by definition of the spectral integral in
Eq.

IV.2
and the identities found above, one computes

\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{0}^{+\infty}\log(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,J\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})J\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\ .

Remark IV.1.4
.

Using the spectral representation (
IV.2
), it becomes apparent why the condition
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
has to be imposed in order for
-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\big\rangle
to be well-defined: it was shown above that
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi=\varPsi
is a consequence of the support condition, hence
\varPsi\in\ker(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})^{\perp}
(
B.1.13
and
Lemma

B.1.7
), and therefore
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\{{0}\})\,\varPsi=0
because
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\{{0}\})
is the orthogonal projection onto
\ker(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
(
Proposition

B.3.4
). Thus, the integral is well-defined at the lower end since the set
\{\lambda=0\}
has
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}
-measure zero. If this condition is not satisfied, then
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(0)\varPsi\neq 0
in general, hence there is a divergent contribution to the integral implying
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=+\infty
.

Before proving important properties of the Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy, it shall first be shown that
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}
is indeed a generalization of the Umegaki and classical relative entropies defined in
Section

I.1
.

Examples IV.1.5
.

(a)

Consider the commutative von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}=L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
for a
\sigma
-finite measure space
(X,\varSigma,\mu)
. Recall from
Examples

III.2.5

(a)
that the standard form is given by
\bigl(\mathfrak{M},L^{2}(X,\mu),J,L^{2}(X,\mu)_{+}\bigr)
, and from
Examples

II.4.8

(a)
that
\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}\cong L^{1}(X,\mu)_{+}
such that to every
\psi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
, one can associate the measure
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\psi}=h_{\psi}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu
,
h_{\psi}\in L^{1}(X,\mu)_{+}
. Furthermore,
Examples

III.3.4

(a)
showed that for every pair of normal functionals
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
, the relative modular operator
\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}
is given by the multiplication operator with the Radon-Nikodým derivative of
\nu_{\varphi}
with respect to
\nu_{\psi}
, restricted to the support of
\psi
. Observe that the existence of
\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}
implies that
\nu_{\varphi}\ll\nu_{\psi}

[
40
, p. 122]
, and that in case
\supp(\psi)\subseteq\supp(\varphi)
, one also has
\nu_{\psi}\ll\nu_{\varphi}
, hence it follows that
[
58
, p. 309]

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\varphi}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\nu_{\psi}}}}}\right)^{-1}=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\psi}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\nu_{\varphi}}}\ .

Let
\varPsi\coloneqq\sqrt{h_{\psi}}\in L^{2}(X,\mu)_{+}
be the vector representative of the functional
\psi
. With the above remarks, the relative entropy of
\psi
with respect to
\varphi
in case
\supp(\psi)\subseteq\supp(\varphi)
takes the form

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi})\varPsi}\big\rangle=-\int_{X}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\varphi}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\nu_{\psi}}}}}\right)\,h_{\psi}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mu=\int_{X}\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\psi}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{\nu_{\varphi}}}}}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\nu_{\psi}=D(\nu_{\psi},\nu_{\varphi})\ .

That is, the Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy of two positive normal functionals on
\mathfrak{M}=L^{\infty}(X,\mu)
reduces to the Kullback-Leibler divergence from
Eq.

I.1
:
D(\mu,\nu)=\mathcal{S}_{L^{\infty}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\omega_{\mu},\omega_{\nu})
.

(b)

Next, consider the von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}=\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
over a separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. According to
Examples

III.2.5

(b)
, the standard form of
\mathfrak{M}
is given by
\bigl(\mathfrak{M},\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H}),J,\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H})_{+}\bigr)
. For all positive normal functionals
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
, there exist positive trace-class operators
\rho_{\psi},\rho_{\varphi}\in\mathscr{B}_{1}(\mathcal{H})_{+}
such that
\varPsi=\sqrt{\rho_{\psi}}
and
\varPhi=\sqrt{\rho_{\varphi}}
are their vector representatives in
\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H})_{+}
.

In
Examples

III.3.4

(b)
, it was shown that if
\rho_{\varphi}=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\lambda_{i}\,P_{i}
and
\rho_{\psi}=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\mu_{j}\,Q_{j}
are the spectral decompositions of
\rho_{\psi}
and
\rho_{\varphi}
, then the relative modular operator
\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}
on
\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H})
is given by the following expression:

\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}=L_{\rho_{\varphi}}R_{\rho_{\psi}^{-1}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{i}\mu_{j}^{-1}L_{P_{i}}R_{Q_{j}}\ .

Here,
L_{T}
and
R_{T}
denote the left and right multiplication operators with the operator
T
, respectively, and
\rho_{\psi}^{-1}
is the generalized inverse. From the functional calculus, one obtains
[
144
, Exa. 5.1]

\displaystyle\log(\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\log(\lambda_{i}\mu_{j}^{-1})L_{P_{i}}R_{Q_{j}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\log(\lambda_{i})L_{P_{i}}R_{Q_{j}}-\sum_{i,j=1}^{\infty}\log(\mu_{j})L_{P_{i}}R_{Q_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\log(\lambda_{i})L_{P_{i}}-\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\log(\mu_{j})R_{Q_{j}}=\log(\rho_{\varphi})-\log(\rho_{\psi})\ ,

where it was used that
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}R_{Q_{j}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H})}
and
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}L_{P_{i}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathscr{B}_{2}(\mathcal{H})}
. With this result, the following expression for the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
in case that
\supp(\rho_{\psi})\subseteq\supp(\rho_{\varphi})
is found:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\big\rangle=-\tr\bigl(\rho_{\psi}^{1/2}\bigl(\log(\rho_{\varphi})-\log(\rho_{\psi})\bigr)\rho_{\psi}^{1/2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\tr\bigl(\rho_{\psi}\log\rho_{\psi}-\rho_{\psi}\log\rho_{\varphi}\bigr)=S(\rho_{\psi},\rho_{\varphi})\ .

That is, the Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy of two positive normal functionals on
\mathfrak{M}=\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
reduces to the Umegaki relative entropy from
Eq.

I.2
:
S(\rho,\sigma)=\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})}^{\mathrm{std}}(\omega_{\rho},\omega_{\sigma})
.

IV.1.b  Fundamental Properties

The subsequent properties of the relative entropy (
Propositions

IV.1.6
,
IV.1.7
,
IV.1.8
and
IV.1.9
) are stated in
Araki
’s paper
[
11
]
, and the proofs given here are more detailed versions of the proofs given in that reference.

Proposition IV.1.6
.

The relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
is well-defined. It takes a finite real value or
+\infty
, and it satisfies the inequality

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\geq-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}}}\right)\ .

(IV.3)

Proof
.

If
\mathbf{s}(\psi)>\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
, it holds that
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=+\infty
by definition, and hence there is nothing to show. Thus, assume that
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
. The relative Tomita operator
S_{\varPsi,\varPhi}
(
III.3.2
) then satisfies
S_{\varPsi,\varPhi}\varPhi=\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\varPsi=\varPsi
because
\varPsi\in\supp(\varphi)
by
Remark

IV.1.2
. Since also
J\varPsi=\varPsi
by
Proposition

III.2.2

(b)
, it follows from
Proposition

III.3.5

(b)
that

\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{1/2}\,\varPhi=JS_{\varPsi,\varPhi}\,\varPhi=\varPsi\ .

Next, recall that
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
is the orthogonal projection onto
\ker(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})^{\perp}
(
B.1.13
), and
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\{0\})
is the projection onto
\ker(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
(
Proposition

B.3.4
). Therefore,
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\{0\})=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{{0}\}}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})
by
Proposition

B.3.3

(d)
. Let
f:\mathbb{R}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathbb{C}\cup\{{+\infty}\}
be an
E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}
-almost everywhere finite Borel-measurable function. Since
\mathbf{1}_{\{{0}\}}
is bounded,
Proposition

B.3.3

(c)
implies

\displaystyle f(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\,\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})

\displaystyle=f(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\bigl(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{{0}\}}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{1}_{\{{0}\}}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\bigr)f\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}\bigr)=\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})f\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}\bigr)\ .

With the above two observations, the fact that
\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi=\varPsi
(which was shown in the proof of
Lemma

IV.1.3
), and
Proposition

B.3.3

(a)
, one can compute the following integral:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int\nolimits_{0}^{+\infty}\lambda^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle&=\big\langle{\varPsi,\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\big\rangle=\langle{\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1/2}\,\varPsi,\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi}^{-1/2}\,\varPsi}\rangle\\
&=\langle{\varPhi,\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPhi}\rangle=\langle{\varPhi,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{\varphi}\varPhi}\rangle=\langle{\varPhi,\mathbf{s}(\psi)\varPhi}\rangle=\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)\\[4.0pt]
&\leq\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\varphi)\bigr)=\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\ .\end{split}

(IV.4)

Using that
\log(\lambda)\leq\lambda
for all
\lambda>0
, it follows that
-\log(\lambda)=\log(\lambda^{-1})\leq\lambda^{-1}
and hence, using the spectral representation of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
from
Lemma

IV.1.3
, one obtains the following bound:

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\log(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle\geq-\int_{0}^{+\infty}\lambda^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle\geq-\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\ .

This shows that the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
is bounded from below, hence it is well-defined, and it takes real values or the value
+\infty
.

It remains to prove
Eq.

IV.3
. Assume still
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
so that
\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{\varphi}\varPsi=\varPsi
. Then it follows from the functional calculus as before that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\varPsi,\mathbf{1}_{(0,+\infty)}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\varPsi,\mathbf{s}(\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPhi})\varPsi}\rangle=\langle{\varPsi,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\mathbf{s}^{\prime}_{\varphi}\varPsi}\rangle=\psi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)=\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\ .

This implies that
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}\varPsi}\rangle/\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})
is a probability measure on
(0,+\infty)
. Using that the logarithm is concave, one can apply Jensen’s inequality
[
58
, Thm. VI.1.3]
and
Eq.

IV.4
to find

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\int\nolimits_{0}^{+\infty}\log(\lambda^{-1})\,\frac{1}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\biggl(\,\int\nolimits_{0}^{+\infty}\lambda^{-1}\,\frac{1}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varPsi,E_{\varPsi,\varPhi}(\lambda)\varPsi}\rangle\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}}}\right)\ .

Proposition IV.1.7
.

The definition of the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
does not depend on the choice of the natural positive cone
\mathcal{P}
and the vector representatives
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
of
\psi
and
\varphi
.

Proof
.

Let
(\mathfrak{M},\mathcal{H},J^{\prime},\mathcal{P}^{\prime})
be another standard form of the von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}
. By
Corollary

III.2.11
, there exists a unique unitary operator
U\in\mathscr{U}(\mathcal{H})
such that (i)
UAU^{\ast}=A
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}
and (ii)
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}=U\mathcal{P}
. If
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
are the vector representatives of
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
in the cone
\mathcal{P}
, then
\varPsi^{\prime}\coloneqq U\varPsi
and
\varPhi^{\prime}\coloneqq U\varPhi
are the vector representatives in the cone
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
by virtue of property (ii). According to
Lemma

III.3.8
, it holds that
\varDelta_{U\varPhi,U\varPsi}=U\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}U^{\ast}
. With this relation, it follows that

\displaystyle-\big\langle{\varPsi^{\prime},\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi^{\prime},\varPsi^{\prime}})\varPsi^{\prime}}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{U\varPsi,\log(U\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}U^{\ast})U\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{U\varPsi,U\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})U^{\ast}U\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\ .

In the second step,
Lemma

B.3.5
was used. This shows that the relative entropy takes the same value for both the representatives
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
and
\varPsi^{\prime},\varPhi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of the functionals
\psi,\varphi
.

Proposition IV.1.8
.

If
\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})=\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})>0
, then
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\geq 0
. Equality
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=0
holds true if and only if
\psi=\varphi
.

Proof
.

Using that
\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)\leq\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})
together with the inequality (
IV.3
) and the assumption
\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})=\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})
, one obtains non-negativity of the relative entropy:

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\geq-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}}}\right)\geq-\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}{\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})}}}\right)=0\ .

Regarding the case of equality, first assume that
\psi=\varphi
. Then
\varPsi=\varPhi
and
\mathbf{s}(\psi)=\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
. The relative modular operator satisfies
\varDelta_{\varPsi,\varPsi}\varPsi=S_{\varPsi}^{\ast}S_{\varPsi}\varPsi=\varPsi
, that is,
\varPsi\in\Eig(\varDelta_{\varPsi},1)
. From this and
Lemma

B.3.6
, it follows that
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=-\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPsi})\varPsi}\rangle=-\langle{\varPsi,\log(1)\varPsi}\rangle=0
. Conversely, if
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=0
, then necessarily
\mathbf{s}(\psi)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
and
\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)=\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})
. (If
\varphi\bigl(\mathbf{s}(\psi)\bigr)<\varphi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})
were to hold true, then
Eq.

IV.3
would imply that
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)>0
, see above.) From the definition of the support projection, it follows that
\mathbf{s}(\psi)=\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
. In this case,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
coincides with the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{N}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)
on the algebra
\mathfrak{N}=\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\mathfrak{M}\mathbf{s}_{\psi}
on which both
\psi
and
\varphi
are faithful
[
11
, Rem. 3.5]
. Since the relative entropy between faithful states is strictly positive
[
10
, Eq. (1.3)]
, it follows that
\psi=\varphi
.

Proposition IV.1.9
.

For
\lambda,\mu>0
, there holds

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\lambda\psi,\mu\varphi)=\lambda\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\lambda\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}}}\right)\ .

Proof
.

A scaling of the functional
\psi=\omega_{\varPsi}
by
\lambda
induces a scaling of the vector representative
\varPsi
by
\lambda^{1/2}
. Therefore, using the identity
\varDelta_{\mu\varphi,\lambda\psi}=\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\,\varDelta_{\varphi,\psi}
from
Lemma

III.3.7
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\lambda\psi,\mu\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\langle{\lambda^{1/2}\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\mu\varphi,\lambda\psi})\lambda^{1/2}\varPsi}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda\,\bigg\langle{\varPsi,\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\,\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}}}\right)\varPsi}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda\,\langle{\varPsi,\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})\varPsi}\rangle-\lambda\,\bigg\langle{\varPsi,\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}}}\right)\varPsi}\bigg\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\lambda\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})\log\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}}}\right)\ .

The next inequality will be used in
Chapter

V
in the perturbation theory of KMS-states. The proof presented here is an adaptation of
[
50
, Thm. 4.3 (4)]
.

Lemma IV.1.10
.

Let
A\in\mathfrak{M}^{\mathrm{sa}}
be a self-adjoint element in the center
\mathfrak{M}\cap\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, and assume that
\psi(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}})=1
. Then the following inequality holds true:

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)\geq\psi(A)-\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A})\bigr)\ .

(IV.5)

Proof
.

One may assume that
\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\leq\mathbf{s}_{\varphi}
since otherwise there is nothing to show. By the assumption on
A
and
Lemma

III.3.6
, it follows that the operators
\mathrm{e}^{A}
and
\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}
commute with each other. Therefore, using the functional calculus for commuting operators
[
144
, Sect. 5.5]
, one obtains

\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})+A-\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)=\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})+\log\bigl(\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)=\log\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)\ .

From the well-known inequality
\log(x)\leq x-1
for positive
x>0
(see
Footnote

3
in
Chapter

IV
on
Footnote

3
for a proof), it follows that

\log\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)\leq\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\ .

Thus, combining the definition (
IV.1
) of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}
and the previous two identities, one finds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\psi(A)+\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\bigl(\log(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi})+A-\log\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\langle{\varPsi,\log\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\big\langle{\varPsi,\bigl(\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})-\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}\bigr)\varPsi}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=-\big\|{\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\varPsi}\big\|^{2}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})+1\ .

Using the relation
S_{\varPhi,\varPsi}=J\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}^{1/2}
(
Proposition

III.3.5

(b)
) and the definition (
III.8
) of the relative Tomita operator
S_{\varPhi,\varPsi}
, one obtains

\varDelta_{\varPhi,\varPsi}^{1/2}\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\varPsi=JS_{\varPhi,\varPsi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\varPsi=J\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\varPhi=J\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\varPhi\ .

(Note that
\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
since
A\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
by assumption.) The norm of the this expression is given by
\|J\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\varPhi\|^{2}=\|\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\varPhi\|^{2}=\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})
because
J
is anti-unitary.
Inserting this result in the above inequality, the assertion follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\psi(A)+\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\psi(A)+\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\varphi})\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)-\psi(A)+\log\bigl(\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq-\|J\,\mathrm{e}^{A/2}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi}\varPhi\|^{2}/\varphi(\mathrm{e}^{A}\,\mathbf{s}_{\psi})+1=0\ .

IV.2
Spatial Form of the Relative Entropy

Having introduced the relative entropy based on the relative modular operator, and having proved several properties of this functional, now, another definition for a relative entropy functional will be given which relies on the spatial derivative operator; it is taken from
[
118
, Eq. (5.1)]
.

IV.2.a  Definition and Relation to the Standard Form

Definition IV.2.1
(Relative entropy – spatial form).

Let
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a von Neumann algebra acting on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, let
\varphi\in\varSigma_{\ast}(\mathfrak{M})
be an arbitrary normal state on
\mathfrak{M}
, and let
\omega=\omega_{\varOmega}\in\varSigma_{\ast}(\mathfrak{M})
be a vector state on the algebra
\mathfrak{M}
induced by a cyclic vector
\varOmega\in\mathcal{H}
for the algebra
\mathfrak{M}
. The
Araki-Uhlmann relative entropy

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)
of the state
\omega
with respect to the state
\varphi
is defined to be

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)\coloneqq\begin{cases}-\big\langle{\varOmega,\log\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})\varOmega}\big\rangle&\text{if $\varOmega\in\supp(\varphi)$}\ ,\\
+\infty&\text{otherwise}\ .\end{cases}

(IV.6)

Here,
\omega^{\prime}=\omega_{\varOmega}^{\prime}
denotes the vector state induced by
\varOmega
on the commutant
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
, and
\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})
is the spatial derivative of
\varphi
with respect to
\omega^{\prime}
which was introduced in
III.4.12
.

Remark IV.2.2
.

Since
\varOmega\in\mathcal{H}
is assumed to be cyclic for
\mathfrak{M}
, it is separating for the commutant
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
by
Proposition

II.3.16

(a)
. Therefore, it follows from
Corollary

II.4.14
that the vector functional
\omega^{\prime}
is faithful on
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. Hence, the spatial derivative
\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})
is well-defined. Similarly to
Remark

IV.1.2
, one shows that the condition
\varOmega\in\supp(\varphi)
is equivalent to
\mathbf{s}(\omega)\leq\mathbf{s}(\varphi)
. Note that it is not clear yet whether the definition of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}
is independent of the vector representative
\varOmega
of the functionals
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
; this will be proved later with the help of
Theorem

IV.3.7
.

The following proposition answers the obvious question regarding the relationship between the functionals
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}
on a von Neumann algebra
\mathfrak{M}
.

Proposition IV.2.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be a von Neumann algebra in standard form
(\mathfrak{M},\mathcal{H},J,\mathcal{P})
and
\psi,\varphi\in\mathfrak{M}_{\ast}^{+}
with vector representatives
\varPsi,\varPhi\in\mathcal{P}
. Assume that
\psi
is faithful on
\mathfrak{M}
, and define a normal functional
\psi^{\prime}\in(\mathfrak{M}^{\prime})_{\ast}^{+}
by
\psi^{\prime}(A^{\prime})\coloneqq\psi(J(A^{\prime})^{\ast}J)
for all
A^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
. Then

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{std}}(\psi,\varphi)=\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\psi,\varphi)\ .

Proof
.

First, note that the functional
\psi^{\prime}
is given by
\psi^{\prime}=\omega^{\prime}_{\varPsi}
on the commutant
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
:

\psi^{\prime}(A^{\prime})=\langle{\varPsi,J(A^{\prime})^{\ast}J\varPsi}\rangle=\langle{(A^{\prime})^{\ast}J\varPsi,J\varPsi}\rangle=\langle{\varPsi,A^{\prime}\varPsi}\rangle=\omega_{\varPsi}^{\prime}(A^{\prime})\ .

Next, observe that since
\psi
is assumed to be faithful, the vector representative
\varPsi\in\mathcal{P}
is cyclic and separating for
\mathfrak{M}
(
Remark

III.2.9
). Therefore,
\varPsi
is also cyclic and separating for the commutant
\mathfrak{M}^{\prime}
by
Proposition

II.3.16
, hence
\psi^{\prime}=\omega^{\prime}_{\varPsi}
is faithful. Thus, the spatial derivative operator
\varDelta(\varphi/\psi^{\prime})
is well-defined, and the claim immediately follows from
Proposition

III.4.13
.

IV.2.b  Uhlmann’s Representation

The following representation of the relative entropy, which was first given by
A. Uhlmann
in 1977 as a
definition
of the relative entropy
[
163
, Eq. (45)]
, is very useful for proving certain properties of this functional; in particular, it will be used in the next section to prove
Uhlmann
’s monotonicity theorem. The following proof is a much more detailed version of the argument given in
[
118
, p. 80]
, and it corrects a mistake in this reference which claims that the convergence of
Eq.

IV.8
is increasing for
\lambda>1
.

Theorem IV.2.4
(Uhlmann’s representation of the relative entropy).

In the setting of
Definition

IV.2.1
, the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)
can be written as follows:

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)=-\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{1}{t}\Bigl(\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega\|^{2}\Bigr)\ .

(IV.7)

Proof
.

1.
Assume first that
\varOmega\in\supp(\varphi)
, and denote by
E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}
the unique spectral measure associated with the operator
\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})
. From
Proposition

B.3.3

(b)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega}\big\|^{2}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\lambda^{t}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\|\varOmega\|^{2}=\int_{0}^{+\infty}\mathbf{1}_{[0,+\infty)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle\ .

Statement
.

Let
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
be a positive number. Then the expression
\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}
,
t>0
, converges monotonically decreasingly towards
\log(\lambda)
as
t\searrow 0
:

\log(\lambda)=\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}\ .

(IV.8)

Proof
(of statement).

It will be shown that (i) the limit of
\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}
is actually given by
\log(\lambda)
, and (ii) that the convergence of this function to
\log(\lambda)
is monotonically decreasing for all
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
.

(i) For every positive
\lambda>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}=\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{\mathrm{e}^{t\log(\lambda)}-\mathrm{e}^{0\cdot\log(\lambda)}}{t}=\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{t}}_{\upharpoonright\,t=0}\,\mathrm{e}^{t\log(\lambda)}=\log(\lambda)\ .

(ii) For
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
, define a function
f_{\lambda}:(0,+\infty)\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathbb{R}
,
t\varmapstochar\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow f_{\lambda}(t)
, by

f_{\lambda}(t)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\int\nolimits_{1}^{\lambda}\xi^{t-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi&\text{if $\lambda\geq 1$}\ ,\\[10.0pt]
\displaystyle-\int\nolimits_{\lambda}^{1}\xi^{t-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi&\text{if $\lambda<1$}\ .\end{cases}

Observe that since
\int_{1}^{\lambda}\xi^{t-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\xi=\frac{1}{t}\,\xi^{t}\big|_{1}^{\lambda}=\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}
, it follows that
f_{\lambda}(t)=\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}
for all
\lambda,t>0
. Let
t_{1},t_{2}\in(0,+\infty)
be arbitrary numbers such that
t_{2}\geq t_{1}>0
. On the one hand, if
\lambda>1
, then for all
\xi\in[1,\lambda]
there holds
\log(\xi)\geq 0
, and hence

\xi^{t_{1}-1}=\mathrm{e}^{(t_{1}-1)\log(\xi)}=\mathrm{e}^{t_{1}\log(\xi)}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\log(\xi)}\leq\mathrm{e}^{t_{2}\log(\xi)}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\log(\xi)}=\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-1)\log(\xi)}=\xi^{t_{2}-1}\ .

On the other hand, if
0<\lambda<1
, then
\log(\xi)<0
for all
\xi\in[\lambda,1]
; therefore

-\xi^{t_{1}-1}=-\mathrm{e}^{(t_{1}-1)\log(\xi)}=-\mathrm{e}^{-t_{1}|\log(\xi)|}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\log(\xi)}\leq-\mathrm{e}^{-t_{2}|\log(\xi)|}\,\mathrm{e}^{-\log(\xi)}=-\mathrm{e}^{(t_{2}-1)\log(\xi)}=-\xi^{t_{2}-1}\ .

These two inequalities show that for all
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
, it holds that

f_{\lambda}(t_{1})\leq f_{\lambda}(t_{2})\ ,

hence
f_{\lambda}
is a monotonically increasing function on
(0,+\infty)
. Therefore, since
\lim_{t\searrow 0}f_{\lambda}(t)=\log(\lambda)
by (i), it follows that
\frac{\lambda^{t}-1}{t}
converges monotonically decreasingly to
\log(\lambda)
as
t\searrow 0
.

The derivation of
Eq.

IV.7
may now be completed. Define a sequence
(t_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq(0,1]
by setting
t_{n}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
; this yields a monotonically decreasing sequence of positive numbers converging towards zero, that is,
t_{n+1}\leq t_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t_{n}\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
; note also that
t_{1}=1
. Next, define for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
a measurable function
f_{n}:(0,+\infty)\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

f_{n}(\lambda)\coloneqq f_{\lambda}(t_{n})=\frac{\lambda^{t_{n}}-1}{t_{n}}\ ,

where
f_{\lambda}
was defined in part (ii) of the proof of the statement. From the properties of
f_{\lambda}
shown there, it follows for all
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
that
f_{n+1}(\lambda)=f_{\lambda}(t_{n+1})\leq f_{\lambda}(t_{n})=f_{n}(\lambda)
and
f_{n}(\lambda)\rightarrow\log(\lambda)
as
n\rightarrow+\infty
, that is,
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a monotonically decreasing sequence converging pointwise to
\log(\lambda)
. Finally,

\int_{0}^{+\infty}f_{1}(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle=\int_{0}^{+\infty}(\lambda-1)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle=\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{1/2}\varOmega}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega\|^{2}<+\infty

by
Proposition

B.3.3

(b)
. The last expression is finite because
\varOmega\in D(\mathcal{H},\omega^{\prime})\subseteq\dom\bigl(\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{1/2}\bigr)
according to the final remarks made in
III.4.12
. The previous observations show that the sequence
(f_{n})_{n}
satisfies all of the assumptions of the monotone convergence theorem for decreasing sequences
[
40
, p. 65]
; with it, one obtains the assertion of the theorem:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{+\infty}\log(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle=-\int_{0}^{+\infty}\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}f_{n}(\lambda)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\frac{\lambda^{t_{n}}-1}{t_{n}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\langle{\varOmega,E_{\varphi,\omega^{\prime}}(\lambda)\varOmega}\rangle=-\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{1}{t}\Bigl(\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega\|^{2}\Bigr)\ .

2.
(
[
50
, Thm. 4.3 (1)]
) Next, consider the case that
\varOmega\notin\supp(\varphi)
. Since according to
III.4.12
the support of the spatial derivative is given by
\supp\bigl(\varDelta(\varphi,\omega^{\prime})\bigr)=\supp(\varphi)
, it follows that
\varOmega=\varOmega_{1}+\varOmega_{2}
, where
\varOmega_{1}\perp\varOmega_{2}
and
\varOmega_{1}\in\ker\bigl(\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})\bigr)\setminus\{0\}
. (Recall that the support of an operator is the projection onto the orthogonal complement of its kernel.) With this, one computes

\displaystyle\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega_{2}}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega_{2}\|^{2}-\|\varOmega_{1}\|^{2}

using the spectral representation of the first term and the Pythagorean theorem
[
171
, Eq. (V.23)]
for the second one. It then follows that

-\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{1}{t}\Bigl(\big\|{\varDelta(\varphi/\omega^{\prime})^{t/2}\varOmega_{2}}\big\|^{2}-\|\varOmega_{2}\|^{2}-\|\varOmega_{1}\|^{2}\Bigr)=\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega_{\varOmega_{2}},\varphi)+\lim_{t\searrow 0}\frac{\|\varOmega_{1}\|^{2}}{t}=+\infty\ .

Thus, also in the case that
\varOmega\notin\supp(\varphi)
, the value of the relative entropy
\mathcal{S}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega,\varphi)
agrees with the one of the expression (
IV.7
), hence the assertion is proved.

IV.3
Uhlmann’s Monotonicity Theorem

The goal of this section is to prove the very general monotonicity theorem for the relative entropy of
A. Uhlmann
. To this end, a technical interpolation lemma is crucially needed which, in turn, relies on certain auxiliary inequalities from the theory of operator monotone functions.

IV.3.a  An Interpolation Inequality

The following inequality is well-known for matrices
[
21
, Thm. V.1.9]
(originally proved by
K. Löwner
(1934)
[
109
]
and
E. Heinz
(1951)
[
80
]
), but it actually holds true for positive self-adjoint operators on Hilbert spaces as well. The proof can be found in
[
144
, Prop. 10.14]
.

Lemma IV.3.1
(Löwner-Heinz inequality).

Let
A
and
B
be two self-adjoint operators on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
. If
A\geq B\geq 0
(in the sense of
C.2.1
), then also
A^{t}\geq B^{t}
for all
t\in(0,1)
.

Introduce the notation
f_{t}:[0,+\infty)\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow[0,+\infty)
,
\lambda\varmapstochar\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\lambda^{t}
. The Löwner-Heinz inequality implies that
f_{t}
is an
operator monotone
function. (See
[
21
,
85
,
151
]
for a systematic treatment of these functions.) The next result was established by
F. Hansen
and
G. K. Pedersen

[
77
,
78
,
79
]
for
any
operator monotone function. Proofs of this result can also be found in
[
21
, Thm. V.2.3]
and
[
118
, Lem. 1.2]
.

Lemma IV.3.2
(Bounded Hansen-Jensen-Pedersen inequality).

Let
\mathcal{H}
be a Hilbert space, let
A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})^{+}
be a bounded positive self-adjoint operator, and let
K\in\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
be a contraction, that is, an operator such that
\|K\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1
. Then

\forall t\in(0,1)\ :\ K^{\ast}f_{t}(A)K\leq f_{t}(K^{\ast}AK)\ .

(IV.9)

With the help of the previous two lemmata, one can prove a generalization of the Hansen-Jensen-Pedersen inequality which is valid for unbounded operators. The proof is a slightly modified version of an argument given by
D. Petz
in
[
122
, Thm. B]
; he uses a similar result as
Lemma

IV.3.1
(which he proves in
[
122
, Thm. A]
but which first appeared in
[
13
, Lem. D]
) to establish the relevant inequality.

Lemma IV.3.3
(Unbounded Hansen-Jensen-Pedersen inequality).

If
T
is a positive self-adjoint operator on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
and
K\in\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
is a contraction, then

\forall t\in(0,1)\ :\ K^{\ast}f_{t}(T)K\leq f_{t}(K^{\ast}TK)\ .

Proof
.

1.
For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, denote by
E_{n}\coloneqq E_{T}([0,n])=\mathbf{1}_{[0,n]}(T)
the spectral projection onto
[0,n]
. Then the following relation (in the sense of
C.2.1
) holds true:

K^{\ast}E_{n}TK\leq K^{\ast}TK

To see this, first, define the closed operators
A\coloneqq T^{1/2}E_{n}K
and
B\coloneqq T^{1/2}K
(
cf.

[
152
, Prop. 8.11]
), and observe that
K^{\ast}E_{n}TK=A^{\ast}A
and
K^{\ast}TK=B^{\ast}B
, hence the claim is equivalent to
A^{\ast}A\leq B^{\ast}B
. Secondly, recall that for every closed operator
C
on
\mathcal{H}
, it holds that
\dom(C)=\dom\bigl((C^{\ast}C)^{1/2}\bigr)
and
\|C\xi\|=\|(C^{\ast}C)^{1/2}\xi\|
for all
\xi\in\dom(C)

[
144
, Lem. 7.1]
. Therefore, the relation
A^{\ast}A\leq B^{\ast}B
is equivalent to
\dom(B)\subseteq\dom(A)
and
\|A\xi\|\leq\|B\xi\|
for all
\xi\in\dom(B)
.

Let
\xi\in\dom(B)=\dom(T^{1/2}K)=\{{\eta\in\mathcal{H}\,:\,K\eta\in\dom(T^{1/2})}\}
be arbitrary. Then clearly
E_{n}K\xi\in\dom(T^{1/2})
, that is,
\xi\in\dom(T^{1/2}E_{n}K)=\dom(A)
. Furthermore, one computes

\displaystyle\|T^{1/2}E_{n}K\xi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|{E_{n}T^{1/2}K\xi}\big\|^{2}+\big\|{(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-E_{n})T^{1/2}K\xi}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|{(E_{n}+\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-E_{n})T^{1/2}K\xi}\big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big\|{T^{1/2}K\xi}\big\|^{2}\ ,

where the fact that functions of
T
commute with the spectral projections of
T
(
Proposition

B.3.3

(c)
) and the Pythagorean theorem
[
171
, Eq. (V.23)]
were used. This proves the claim.

2.
Applying now the result of
Lemma

IV.3.1
, one obtains
f_{t}(K^{\ast}E_{n}TK)\leq f_{t}(K^{\ast}TK)
, where as before
f_{t}
denotes the function
\lambda\varmapstochar\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\lambda^{t}
,
t\in(0,1)
. Therefore, using this inequality and
Lemma

IV.3.2
applied to the bounded operator
E_{n}T
, one obtains

K^{\ast}f_{t}(E_{n}T)K\leq f_{t}(K^{\ast}E_{n}TK)\leq f_{t}(K^{\ast}TK)\ .

(IV.10)

3.
Finally, observe that the operator
f_{t}(E_{n}T)
is given as follows,
cf.

Proposition

B.3.3

(c)
:

f_{t}(E_{n}T)=\bigl(f_{t}\circ(\mathbf{1}_{[0,n]}\mathrm{Id}_{\sigma(T)})\bigr)(T)=\begin{cases}f_{t}(T)&\text{on $[0,n]$}\ ,\\
f_{t}(0)&\text{on $\sigma(T)\setminus[0,n]$}\ .\end{cases}

Thus, the left-hand side of (
IV.10
) can be written as

K^{\ast}f_{t}(E_{n}T)K=K^{\ast}f_{t}(T)E_{n}K+K^{\ast}f_{t}(0)(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}-E_{n})K\ .

In the limit
n\rightarrow+\infty
, the projection
E_{n}
converges strongly to
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}}
by properties of the functional calculus
[
156
, Thm. 2.20]
. Thus,
K^{\ast}f_{t}(E_{n}T)K\rightarrow K^{\ast}f_{t}(T)K
by the above identity. Therefore, taking this limit in
Eq.

IV.10
proves the statement.

With these preliminary observations, the main result of this subsection can be proved. The structure of the following proof is the one outlined in
[
118
, Lem. 5.2]
.

Lemma IV.3.4
(Interpolation inequality).

For
i\in\{{1,2}\}
, let
A_{i}
be a positive self-adjoint operator on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{i}
. Assume that
T:\mathcal{H}_{1}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}
is a bounded linear operator with the properties

(
\alpha
)

T\bigl(\dom(A_{1})\bigr)\subseteq\dom(A_{2})
;

(
\beta
)

\forall\xi\in\dom(A_{1}):\|A_{2}T\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\leq\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|A_{1}\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}
.

Then for every
0<t<1
and
\xi\in\dom(A_{1}^{t})
, it holds that

\|A_{2}^{t}T\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\leq\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\|A_{1}^{t}\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}\ .

(IV.11)

Proof
.

Without loss of generality, one may assume that
\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1
; otherwise just rescale the bounded operator
T
by
\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{-1}
. The two assumptions
(

α

)
and
(

β

)
together, and the conclusion of the lemma are, respectively, equivalent to the two operator relations

T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T\leq A_{1}^{2}\quad\text{and}\quad T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2t}T\leq A_{1}^{2t}\ ,

in the sense of
C.2.1
. Indeed, the relation
T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T\leq A_{1}^{2}
is equivalent to
(A_{2}T)^{\ast}(A_{2}T)\leq A_{1}^{\ast}A_{1}
, and, as argued at the beginning of the proof of
Lemma

IV.3.3
, this relation is, itself, equivalent to
\dom(A_{1})\subseteq\dom(A_{2}T)
and
\|A_{2}T\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\leq\|A_{1}\xi\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}
for all
\xi\in\dom(A_{1})
. These two relations are evidently equivalent to the assumptions
(

α

)
and
(

α

)
in the case
\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}=1
. The argument showing that
T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2t}T\leq A_{1}^{2t}
is equivalent to the conclusion of the lemma proceeds analogously.

Using again the notation
f_{t}(\lambda)=\lambda^{t}
for
t\in(0,1)
,
Lemma

IV.3.1
, and the hypothesis
T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T\leq A_{1}^{2}
, it follows that
f_{t}(T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T)\leq f_{t}(A_{1}^{2})
in the sense of the functional calculus, that is,

(T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T)^{t}\leq A_{1}^{2t}\ .

Furthermore, since the operator
T\in\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2})
is a contraction by assumption,
Lemma

IV.3.3
implies that
T^{\ast}f_{t}(A_{2}^{2})T\leq f_{t}(T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T)
,
i.e.
,
T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2t}T\leq(T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2}T)^{t}
. This, together with the above inequality, shows

T^{\ast}A_{2}^{2t}T\leq A_{1}^{2t}
which is the assertion of the lemma.

Remark IV.3.5
.

There are other ways to obtain
Lemma

IV.3.4
. On the one hand, one can use a theorem of
H. Triebel

[
162
, Thm. 1.18.10]
and the Calderón-Lions interpolation theorem
[
133
, Thm. IX.20]
to prove (
IV.11
); an argument along these lines is given in
Petz
’ paper
[
123
, Prop. 1]
. Another different argument leading to
Eq.

IV.11
, based on analyticity properties and the so-called three-line theorem
[
133
, p. 33]
, is presented in
[
50
, Thm. A.2]
.

The next inequality is also of importance for proving the monotonicity theorem; essentially, it is a special case of a general inequality for 2-positive maps mentioned in
Section

II.2.b
. The proof given below follows
[
122
, Thm. E]
, but once again more details are provided.

Lemma IV.3.6
.

Let
\alpha:\mathfrak{A}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathfrak{B}
be a unital 2-positive mapping between two unital
C^{\ast}
-algebras, and let
Q\in\mathfrak{A}
and
P\in\mathfrak{B}
be projections such that
P\leq\alpha(Q)
. Then for all
A\in\mathfrak{A}
, it holds that

\alpha(A)P\alpha(A)^{\ast}\leq\alpha(AQA^{\ast})\ .

(IV.12)

Proof
.

1.
For every
\lambda>0
, define an operator
C_{\lambda}\coloneqq Q+\lambda(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{A}}-Q)\in\mathfrak{A}
. Since
Q
and
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{A}}-Q
are projections in
\mathfrak{A}
,
C_{\lambda}
is invertible with
C_{\lambda}^{-1}=Q+\frac{1}{\lambda}\,(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{A}}-Q)
. Therefore,
Lemma

II.1.10
shows that
C_{\lambda}\geq\varepsilon\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{A}}
for some
\varepsilon>0
, and hence also
\alpha(C_{\lambda})\geq\varepsilon\,\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}
; this implies that
\alpha(C_{\lambda})
is invertible, too. From
Proposition

II.2.9
, it now follows that

\alpha(A)\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1}\alpha(A)^{\ast}\leq\alpha(AC_{\lambda}^{-1}A^{\ast})

(IV.13)

for all
A\in\mathfrak{A}
. In the limit
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, it holds that
C_{\lambda}^{-1}\rightarrow Q
in the operator norm. Therefore, the right-hand side of the above inequality tends to
\alpha(AQA^{\ast})
since positive mappings are uniformly continuous, see
Proposition

II.2.4
.

2.
To analyze the left-hand side of the inequality, first, note that
P
and
\alpha(Q)
commute with each other. To see this, assume that
\mathfrak{B}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H})
for some Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
(
Theorem

II.4.21
), and note that from the assumption
P\leq\alpha(Q)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}
, it follows that
\ran(P)\subseteq\ran(\alpha(Q))
, hence
\alpha(Q)\xi=\xi
for all
\xi\in\ran(P)
by
Lemma

B.1.7
. From this, one obtains for any
\eta\in\mathcal{H}
, which can be decomposed as
\eta=\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}
with
\eta_{1}\in\ran(P)
,
\eta_{2}\in\ran(P)^{\perp}
(
Theorem

B.1.8
), that
\alpha(Q)P\eta=\alpha(Q)\eta_{1}=\eta_{1}
. This shows that
\alpha(Q)P=P
on
\mathcal{H}
, and hence, by taking adjoints, also
P\alpha(Q)=P
since
\alpha
is a
\ast
-preserving map according to
Proposition

II.2.3
. Thus,
\alpha(Q)P=P\alpha(Q)
. In particular, this property implies that
P
also commutes with the element
\alpha(C_{\lambda})
.

3.
Next, observe that from the assumption
P\leq\alpha(Q)\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{A}}
, the fact that
P(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P)=0
, and the Schwarz inequality for 2-positive maps (
Proposition

II.2.12
), it follows that

\displaystyle P\alpha(C_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=P\alpha(Q)+\lambda P\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\alpha(Q)\bigr)\leq\alpha(Q)^{2}+\lambda P(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P)\leq\alpha(Q^{2})\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}\ .

This shows that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-P\alpha(C_{\lambda})=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\alpha(C_{\lambda})P\geq 0
. Furthermore, note that
\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1}\geq 0
as well since
\sigma(\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1})=\big\{{\lambda^{-1}\,:\,\lambda\in\sigma(\alpha(C_{\lambda}))}\big\}

[
29
, Prop. 2.2.3]
and
\alpha(C_{\lambda})\geq 0
.
Lemma

II.1.8
thus implies

\displaystyle\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1}-P=\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1}\bigl(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{B}}-\alpha(C_{\lambda})P\bigr)\geq 0\ ,

showing that
P\leq\alpha(C_{\lambda})^{-1}
. Using this inequality in the result (
IV.13
) obtained above, one arrives, after taking the limit
\lambda\rightarrow+\infty
, at the desired inequality.

IV.3.b  Proof of Uhlmann’s Theorem

The necessary tools to prove the main theorem of this section are assembled. The proof is guided by
[
118
, Thm. 5.3]
and
[
123
, Thm. 2]
but contains more details. Originally, this theorem was proved by
A. Uhlmann
in the paper
[
163
, Prop. 18]
in 1977 using the language of abstract interpolation theory.

Theorem IV.3.7
(Uhlmann’s monotonicity theorem).

For
i\in\{{1,2}\}
, let
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\subseteq\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{H}_{i})
be a von Neumann algebra acting on a Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{i}
and
\varphi_{i},\omega_{i}\in(\mathfrak{M}_{i})_{\ast}^{+}
. Assume that
\omega_{i}=\omega_{\varOmega_{i}}
is given by a cyclic vector
\varOmega_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{i}
for the algebra
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
, and that
\omega_{1}(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}})=\omega_{2}(\mathrm{Id}_{\mathcal{H}_{2}})
. Let
\alpha:\mathfrak{M}_{1}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathfrak{M}_{2}
be a Schwarz mapping such that the following relations hold on
\mathfrak{M}_{1}
:

\omega_{2}\circ\alpha\leq\omega_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{2}\circ\alpha\leq\varphi_{1}\ ,

(IV.14)

that is,
\omega_{2}(\alpha(A))\leq\omega_{1}(A)
and
\varphi_{2}(\alpha(A))\leq\varphi_{1}(A)
for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}_{1}
. Finally, assume that at least one of the following two conditions is satisfied in addition to the previous assumptions:

(U1)

the vector
\varOmega_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{i}
is separating for the algebra
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
;

(U2)

the mapping
\alpha
is 2-positive.

Under these assumptions, the relative entropy satisfies the following inequality:

\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}_{1}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega_{1},\varphi_{1})\leq\mathcal{S}_{\mathfrak{M}_{2}}^{\mathrm{spa}}(\omega_{2},\varphi_{2})\ .

(IV.15)

Proof
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{1}\coloneqq\mathfrak{M}_{1}\varOmega_{1}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{1}
and observe that this defines a dense subset of the Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{1}
due to the assumption that
\varOmega_{1}
is cyclic for
\mathfrak{M}_{1}
. Define an operator
T_{0}:\mathcal{H}_{1}\supseteq\mathcal{D}_{1}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}
by

T_{0}(A\varOmega_{1})\coloneqq\alpha(A)\varOmega_{2}\quad(A\in\mathfrak{M}_{1})\ .

Statement 1
.

The operator
T_{0}
is well-defined and extends to a contraction
T:\mathcal{H}_{1}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}
.

Proof
(of statement).

Using the Schwarz inequality (
II.4
) for
\alpha
and the assumption (
IV.14
) on the states
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{2}
, one finds the following inequality for all
A\in\mathfrak{M}_{1}
:

\displaystyle\|\alpha(A)\varOmega_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\omega_{2}\bigl(\alpha(A)^{\ast}\alpha(A)\bigr)\leq\omega_{2}\bigl(\alpha(A^{\ast}A)\bigr)\leq\omega_{1}(A^{\ast}A)=\|A\varOmega_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}^{2}\ .

This shows that
T_{0}
is a contraction,
i.e.
,
\|T_{0}\|_{\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2})}\leq 1
. (Due to the linearity of Schwarz mappings,
T_{0}
is clearly a linear operator on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
.) Moreover, the above inequality shows that
T_{0}
is well-defined: if
A,B\in\mathfrak{M}_{1}
such that
A\varOmega_{1}=B\varOmega_{1}
, then
(A-B)\varOmega_{1}=0
and hence

\|\alpha(A-B)\varOmega_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}^{2}\leq\|(A-B)\varOmega_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}^{2}=0\ .

From linearity of
\alpha
, it now follows that
\bigl(\alpha(A)-\alpha(B)\bigr)\varOmega_{2}=0
, thus
T_{0}(A\varOmega_{1})=T_{0}(B\varOmega_{1})
. This shows that
T_{0}
is well-defined. By the bounded linear transformation theorem (
Theorem

B.1.1
),
T_{0}
extends to a contraction
T:\mathcal{H}_{1}\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{2}
with
T|_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}=T_{0}
and
\|T\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|T_{0}\|_{\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{H}_{2})}\leq 1
.

Denote by
\varDelta_{i}
the spatial derivative
\varDelta(\varphi_{i}/\omega_{i}^{\prime})
, where
\omega_{i}^{\prime}
is the vector state on
\mathfrak{M}_{i}^{\prime}
induced by
\varOmega_{i}
. Since
\varOmega_{i}\in\mathcal{H}_{i}
is cyclic for
\mathfrak{M}_{i}
,
\varDelta_{i}
is well-defined,
cf.

Remark

IV.2.2
. Furthermore, let
\mathfrak{q}_{i}:\mathcal{H}_{i}\supseteq\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{i})\relbar\joinrel\varrightarrow\mathbb{C}
be the quadratic form
\mathfrak{q}_{i}\coloneqq\mathfrak{q}_{\varphi_{i}}=\varphi_{i}\bigl(\varTheta^{\omega_{i}^{\prime}}(\,\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{.75}{$\bullet$}}}}\,)\bigr)
from which
\varDelta_{i}
was constructed, see
Lemma

III.4.11
. Observe that the space
\mathfrak{M}_{i}\varOmega_{i}
is a core for
\varDelta_{i}^{1/2}
; this follows from the observations at the end of
III.4.12
and the fact that, according to
Lemma

III.4.7
,
\dom(\mathfrak{q}_{i})=D(\mathcal{H},\omega_{i}^{\prime})=\mathfrak{M}_{i}\varOmega_{i}
.

Statement 2
.

It holds that
T\bigl(\dom(\varDelta_{1}^{1/2})\bigr)\subseteq\dom(\varDelta_{2}^{1/2})
and
\big\|{\varDelta_{2}^{1/2}\,T\xi}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{2}}\leq\big\|{\varDelta_{1}^{1/2}\xi}\big\|_{\mathcal{H}_{1}}
for all
\xi\in\dom(\varDelta_{1}^{1/2})
.

Proof
(of statement).

Let
\xi\in\dom(\varDelta_{1}^{1/2})
be arbitrary. By the previous observation (
cf.

Definition

B.2.1
), there exists a sequence
(\xi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{H}_{1}
given by
\xi_{n}=A_{n}\varOmega_{1}
,
A_{n}\in\mathfrak{M}_{1}
